# TorchTomo

**Differentiable 2D computed tomography in PyTorch — from projection to learned reconstruction and geometry calibration.**

[![PyPI](https://img.shields.io/pypi/v/torchtomo.svg?cacheSeconds=300)](https://pypi.org/project/torchtomo/)
[![Python](https://img.shields.io/pypi/pyversions/torchtomo)](https://pypi.org/project/torchtomo/)
[![Tests](https://github.com/itu-biai/torchtomo/actions/workflows/test.yml/badge.svg)](https://github.com/itu-biai/torchtomo/actions/workflows/test.yml)
[![License](https://img.shields.io/badge/license-CC%20BY--NC%204.0-blue.svg)](LICENSE)

TorchTomo provides parallel-beam and flat-detector fan-beam projectors, exact
discrete adjoints, and filtered backprojection (FBP). Use the same API on **CPU,
NVIDIA CUDA, and Apple Silicon (MPS)**, with gradients through images, sinograms,
and scan geometry. The default backend uses PyTorch operations; optional CUDA
kernels compile at first use with no separate build step.

[Quick start](#quick-start) · [Experimental 3D](#experimental-3d-cone-beam) · [Operators](#operators-and-tensor-shapes) ·
[Geometry gradients](#learn-the-scan-geometry) · [Backends](#choose-a-backend) ·
[Benchmarks](#benchmarks-and-examples) · [Implementation notes](docs/advanced.md)

![Shepp–Logan phantom, parallel-beam sinogram, and TorchTomo filtered backprojection](docs/images/reconstruction.png)

*256 × 256 Shepp–Logan phantom, 180 parallel-beam views, ramp-filtered reconstruction.
Generated with TorchTomo’s CPU backend.*

## Why TorchTomo?

- **Start with two dependencies:** PyTorch and NumPy; `pip install torchtomo` installs the library.
- **Build reconstruction networks:** batched `nn.Module` projectors and autograd support for learned primal-dual and other unrolled methods.
- **Use a matched operator pair:** `adjoint()` is the transpose of the implemented `forward()`, up to floating-point roundoff in exact mode.
- **Optimize the scanner geometry:** learn view angles, detector shifts, and fan-beam source shifts and distances as tensors.
- **Accelerate on CUDA:** optional runtime-compiled kernels for forward projection, adjoint, FBP, and first-order geometry gradients.
- **Explore 3D helical CT:** experimental cone beam forward/adjoint operators and an optional CT-PD reader.
- **Experiment immediately:** built-in phantoms and six FBP filters: `ramp`, `shepp-logan`, `cosine`, `hamming`, `hann`, and `none`.

## Installation

Requires **Python 3.10+**. Install a PyTorch build appropriate for your device, then:

```bash
pip install torchtomo
```

The default `backend="torch"` needs no compiler. The optional CUDA backend uses
NVRTC from a CUDA-enabled PyTorch installation; it does not require a separate
`nvcc` build or an additional Python dependency.

## Quick start

### Parallel beam

```python
import torch
from torchtomo import ParallelBeam, shepp_logan

device = torch.device("cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu")
image = shepp_logan(size=256, device=device)  # [1, 1, 256, 256]
projector = ParallelBeam(
    img_size=256, n_angles=180, n_det=256, backend="auto"
).to(device)

sinogram = projector(image)                          # [1, 1, 180, 256]
reconstruction = projector.fbp(sinogram, filter_name="ramp")
print(reconstruction.shape)                          # torch.Size([1, 1, 256, 256])
```

To use Apple Silicon, set `device = torch.device("mps")` when
`torch.backends.mps.is_available()`. Move both the projector and its inputs to
the same device and dtype.

### Fan beam

```python
from torchtomo import FanBeam, shepp_logan

image = shepp_logan(size=256)
projector = FanBeam(img_size=256, n_angles=360, backend="auto")
sinogram = projector(image)                          # [1, 1, 360, 384]
reconstruction = projector.fbp(sinogram, filter_name="hann")
```

Fan beam uses a flat detector. At size 256, the defaults are 384 detector bins
and source-to-isocentre and isocentre-to-detector distances of 512 pixels.
Set `n_det`, `src_dist`, `det_dist`, and `det_spacing` or `det_width` for a
specific acquisition.

## Experimental 3D Cone Beam

`ConeBeam` integrates a 3D volume along explicit source-to-detector segments and
provides their matched discrete adjoint. Source positions have shape
`[views, 3]`; detector element positions have shape `[views, rows, columns, 3]`.
Both use `(x, y, z)` coordinates. The volume shape and voxel spacing use
`(z, y, x)` order. Distances use the same physical unit throughout, and volume
values are attenuation coefficients in its inverse.

```python
from torchtomo import ConeBeam

projector = ConeBeam(
    volume_shape=(32, 128, 128),
    source_positions=sources,
    detector_positions=detector_elements,
    voxel_spacing=(4.0, 3.90625, 3.90625),
    volume_center=(0.0, 0.0, scan_center_z),
    n_samples=192,
    backend="auto",
)
projections = projector(volume)          # [B, 1, views, rows, columns]
adjoint_volume = projector.adjoint(projections)  # [B, 1, Z, Y, X]
reconstruction = projector.fdk(projections)      # flat panels only
```

Provide `sources`, `detector_elements`, and `scan_center_z` from the acquisition
geometry. The same interface handles circular and helical trajectories, flat and
curved detectors, and per-view source offsets. Gradients reach both the volume
and source/detector coordinate tensors. `backward()` aliases `adjoint()`.

`backend="cuda"` (or `"auto"`) runs float32 CUDA tensors on runtime-compiled
kernels: an exact matched forward and adjoint pair, with the geometry gradient
from a kernel of its own, storing nothing per sample. For a 256³ Shepp-Logan
phantom and 360 views of 384 × 384 on an RTX 2080 Ti, a forward projection takes
0.26 s, the adjoint 0.33 s, and a forward with the gradient of every source and
detector position 0.40 s, against 207 s, 232 s, and out of memory on the PyTorch
path ([comparison with ASTRA and LEAP](https://github.com/itu-biai/torchtomo-benchmark/blob/main/libraries/README.md#3d-cone-beam-and-breast-tomosynthesis)).
The default `"torch"` path integrates trilinearly in chunks of `ray_chunk` rays
on any device and dtype; its autograd retains interpolation stencils, so geometry
gradients there fit only small scans.

`fdk(projections, filter_name="ramp")` is Feldkamp-Davis-Kress reconstruction for
flat panels, with its weights derived from the explicit positions: views must be
in orbit order, and the filter runs along the detector axis the source moves
along. An orbit that closes a full turn is halved; shorter scans get no Parker
weights. It runs on both paths and is differentiable in the projections and, on
the PyTorch path, in the geometry. On the scan above it takes 58 ms on the
kernels, against 44 ms for ASTRA's FDK, and agrees with ASTRA's to 0.04%. The
[cone beam examples](benchmark/README.md) include a synthetic check and a TCIA
helical reconstruction pilot. The optional CT-PD reader requires
`pip install 'torchtomo[tcia]'`; it currently accepts uncompressed unsigned
16-bit, single-spectrum, cylindrical-detector data. Measured-data reconstruction
remains experimental.

`torchtomo.ctpd.read_projection(path)` returns a decoded view and its detector
line integrals. The view's `positions(rows, columns)` method generates physical
source and detector coordinates in the CT-PD manual's Cartesian frame.

### Digital breast tomosynthesis

`BreastTomosynthesis` builds a `ConeBeam` for a limited-angle arc: the source
moves on a circle about an axis between it and a stationary flat detector. Angle
0 puts the source above `(0, source_y)`, and positive angles move it toward `+x`,
the direction of increasing detector rows. `angular_range` is the angle from the
first view to the last, both included.

```python
import math

from torchtomo import BreastTomosynthesis

projector = BreastTomosynthesis(
    volume_shape=(57, 336, 408),     # (Z, Y, X)
    n_angles=25,
    angular_range=math.radians(50),
    detector_shape=(752, 384),       # (rows along X, columns along Y)
    pixel_size=0.34,
    source_to_detector=650.0,
    source_to_axis=600.0,
    source_y=-65.28,                 # above the chest-wall edge of the detector
    voxel_spacing=(1.0, 0.34, 0.34),
    volume_center=(x_center, y_center, z_center),
    n_samples=192,
)
```

The projector is differentiable in the volume, in its projections through
`adjoint()`, and in its geometry. `learnable_geometry=True` registers `angles`
(one per view) and `distances` (`source_to_detector`, `source_to_axis`,
`source_y`) as parameters, and positions are rebuilt on every call so optimiser
steps take effect at once. To calibrate only part of the geometry, zero the other
gradients or call `requires_grad_(True)` on one buffer of a fixed projector.

`torchtomo.victre` holds the published geometry of the
[TCIA VICTRE](https://www.cancerimagingarchive.net/collection/victre/) trial and
the projection conversion of the BIAI Lab DBT 2026v1 dataset.
`victre_projector(density, bin_factor=4)` returns the projector on that
dataset's grid, `read_projections(folder)` reads the 25 stored views, and
`convert_projections(views)` returns line integrals, a valid-ray mask, and the
per-view air levels. The readers need `pip install 'torchtomo[tcia]'`.
`backend="cuda"` selects `ConeBeam`'s kernels, including for the geometry
gradient. `fdk(projections, "hann")` filters along tube travel and backprojects;
on a VICTRE phantom it correlates 0.885 with the dataset's own FBP, as the
dataset's filter followed by `adjoint()` does. Like `ConeBeam`, it is
experimental; [`benchmark/dbt`](benchmark/README.md#breast-tomosynthesis)
reconstructs one VICTRE phantom with it.

## Operators and tensor shapes

The 2D projectors use square, single-channel images; the leading dimension batches independent slices.

| Tensor | Shape |
| --- | --- |
| Image | `[batch, 1, img_size, img_size]` |
| Sinogram | `[batch, 1, n_angles, n_det]` |
| Parallel-beam pose | `[n_angles, 2]`: angle, detector shift |
| Fan-beam pose | `[n_angles, 3]`: angle, detector shift, source shift |
| Fan-beam distances | `[3]`: source distance, detector distance, detector width |

| Operation | Meaning | Typical use |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `projector(image)` / `forward(image)` | Forward projection, $A x$ | Simulate measurements; compute data consistency |
| `adjoint(sinogram)` / `backward(sinogram)` | Exact discrete transpose, $A^T y$, in exact mode | Iterative reconstruction; learned primal-dual updates |
| `backproject(sinogram)` | Analytical backprojection with angular normalization and geometry weights | Analytical reconstruction |
| `fbp(sinogram, filter_name="ramp")` | Filtering followed by analytical backprojection | Reconstruct a sinogram; initialize a network |

The discrete adjoint satisfies

```math
\langle A x, y \rangle = \langle x, A^T y \rangle
```

up to floating-point roundoff. Analytical `backproject()` includes different
interpolation and normalization, so use `adjoint()` when an algorithm requires
the transpose of the forward operator.

Angles are in **radians**, and lateral shifts and fan-beam distances are in
**pixels**. Default views cover `[0, π)` for parallel beam and `[0, 2π)` for fan
beam, without repeating the endpoint. Supply `angles=` for explicit views.
`circle=True` masks the image to its inscribed circle; fan-beam rays remain
clipped to the unit circle even with `circle=False`.

## Differentiate through reconstruction

Both projection and adjoint operations participate in autograd. This example
computes an image gradient from a measurement-space loss:

```python
import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F
from torchtomo import ParallelBeam, shepp_logan

projector = ParallelBeam(img_size=64, n_angles=90)
measurements = projector(shepp_logan(size=64)).detach()
image = torch.zeros(1, 1, 64, 64, requires_grad=True)

loss = F.mse_loss(projector(image), measurements)
loss.backward()
print(image.grad.shape)  # torch.Size([1, 1, 64, 64])
```

`projector.adjoint(y)` also differentiates with respect to `y`, so gradients
reach the dual network in a learned primal-dual update. `projector.backward(y)`
is its equivalent operator method; `loss.backward()` is PyTorch’s autograd call.

## Learn the scan geometry

Each view of a 2D projector has a pose tensor. With `learnable_geometry=True`, it becomes an
`nn.Parameter` that an optimizer can update. Fan beam also registers its
`distances` tensor as a parameter.

```python
import torch
from torchtomo import ParallelBeam, shepp_logan

reference = ParallelBeam(img_size=64, n_angles=90)
reference.set_pose(detector_shift=3.0)
measurements = reference(shepp_logan(size=64)).detach()

projector = ParallelBeam(img_size=64, n_angles=90, learnable_geometry=True)
optimizer = torch.optim.Adam(projector.parameters(), lr=0.05)

optimizer.zero_grad()
reconstruction = projector.fbp(measurements)
loss = (projector(reconstruction) - measurements).square().mean()
loss.backward()
print(projector.pose.grad.shape)  # torch.Size([90, 2])
optimizer.step()
```

This demonstrates one optimization step. For a full centre-of-rotation recovery
experiment, run [`benchmark/calibrate_geometry.py`](benchmark/calibrate_geometry.py).
For measured scans and motion correction, see the
[geometry benchmarks](https://github.com/itu-biai/torchtomo-benchmark/tree/main/geometry).

Fixed calibrated geometry can be written with `set_pose(...)` and, for fan
beam, `set_distances(...)`. Pose and distances are saved in the state dict.
See [the geometry notes](docs/advanced.md#differentiable-geometry) for gradients,
checkpoint compatibility, and measured calibration results.

## Choose a backend

| Backend | Geometries | Execution |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `"torch"` (default) | Parallel, fan, cone, tomosynthesis | PyTorch operations on CPU, CUDA, or MPS |
| `"auto"` | Parallel, fan, cone, tomosynthesis | Selects `"cuda"` when NVRTC loads; otherwise `"torch"` |
| `"cuda"` | Parallel, fan, cone, tomosynthesis | Runtime-compiled kernels for float32 CUDA tensors; PyTorch fallback for other devices/dtypes or unavailable NVRTC |
| `"triton"` | Parallel | Optional fused Triton kernels where supported; PyTorch fallback otherwise |

```python
from torchtomo import ParallelBeam

projector = ParallelBeam(img_size=512, n_angles=360, backend="auto")
print(projector.backend)  # Backend selected at construction
```

The CUDA backend compiles kernels on first use and caches them under
`~/.cache/torchtomo`. Set `TORCHTOMO_KERNEL_CACHE` to override the directory;
an empty value disables the disk cache. Geometry gradients on CUDA kernels
support first-order differentiation; use the PyTorch path for higher orders.

`approximate=True` with `"cuda"` or `"auto"` trades the exact transpose pair for
faster texture interpolation and pixel-driven backprojection. Keep the default
`False` for algorithms that depend on adjoint consistency. The
[implementation notes](docs/advanced.md) explain this tradeoff, Triton geometry
fallbacks, sparse adjoints, and the parallel-beam grid-cache budget.

## Benchmarks and examples

Recorded **0.4.0** operator timings: 512 × 512, batch 4, 360 views, RTX 2080 Ti,
PyTorch 2.4.0+cu121. Mean milliseconds per call after three warmups, over 20 calls:

| Geometry | Backend | Forward | Adjoint | FBP |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Parallel | PyTorch | 17.852 | 37.426 | 14.292 |
| Parallel | CUDA, exact | 2.196 | 1.963 | 0.635 |
| Fan | PyTorch | 16.089 | 50.901 | 14.891 |
| Fan | CUDA, exact | 2.720 | 2.983 | 1.095 |

These are measurements on one setup. Find the configurations, raw JSON,
reconstruction figures, and comparisons with LEAP, torch-radon, and ASTRA in
[torchtomo-benchmark’s 0.4.0 results](https://github.com/itu-biai/torchtomo-benchmark/blob/main/libraries/results/0.4.0/README.md).

The core operator experiments use the library’s own dependencies:

```bash
make benchmark-speed      # Available devices and backends
make benchmark-adjoint    # 500 inner-product pairs in float64
make benchmark-calibrate  # Recover a simulated 3 px axis offset
make benchmark-angles     # Learn a 12-view sampling pattern
```

See [`benchmark/README.md`](benchmark/README.md) for details. The companion
[torchtomo-benchmark](https://github.com/itu-biai/torchtomo-benchmark) repository
also provides classical, supervised, and self-supervised reconstruction
pipelines on ellipse phantoms and CT slices, plus measured-scan calibration.

## Upgrading existing code

- **`backward()` computes the exact discrete adjoint.** Use `backproject()` to retain the earlier analytical-backprojection behavior. Models trained with the earlier operator may need retraining or step-size retuning.
- **Version 0.3 changed the FBP ramp filter.** FBP-based results from older versions are not directly comparable with current results.
- **Version 0.4 adds differentiable geometry.** Older checkpoints that stored only `angles` still load with `strict=True`.

Details and the recorded before/after measurements are in the
[migration and implementation notes](docs/advanced.md).

```

Bug reports and contributions are welcome through
[GitHub issues](https://github.com/itu-biai/torchtomo/issues) and pull requests.
For an operator issue, include your TorchTomo/PyTorch versions, device, dtype,
geometry, backend, and a minimal reproducible example. CI tests Python 3.10–3.13;
release publishing is configured in [`.github/workflows/`](.github/workflows).

### src/torchtomo/__init__.py
```
"""
TorchTomo: Differentiable CT Reconstruction in Pure PyTorch

A lightweight library for CT forward and back projection that works
on any device (CPU, CUDA, MPS) without compilation.

Example:
    >>> from torchtomo import ParallelBeam, FanBeam
    >>>
    >>> # Parallel beam
    >>> projector = ParallelBeam(img_size=256, n_angles=180, n_det=256)
    >>> sinogram = projector.forward(image)
    >>> recon = projector.fbp(sinogram)
    >>>
    >>> # Fan beam
    >>> projector = FanBeam(img_size=256, n_angles=360)
    >>> sinogram = projector.forward(image)
    >>> recon = projector.fbp(sinogram)
"""

from .conebeam import ConeBeam
from .dbt import BreastTomosynthesis
from .fanbeam import FanBeam
from .filters import apply_filter, get_filter
from .parallel import ParallelBeam
from .phantom import circle_phantom, shepp_logan

__version__ = "0.5.0"
__all__ = [
    "ParallelBeam",
    "FanBeam",
    "ConeBeam",
    "BreastTomosynthesis",
    "apply_filter",
    "get_filter",
    "shepp_logan",
    "circle_phantom",
]
```

### src/torchtomo/_cone_kernels.cu
```
// Cone beam kernels for ConeBeam, compiled at run time by NVRTC (see _nvrtc.py).
//
// A ray runs from its view's source to one detector element. It is clipped to the
// volume's box, the outer faces of its outermost voxels, and sampled at the
// midpoints of n equal steps; the projection is the step length times the sum of
// the trilinear samples, with zero outside the volume. That is the PyTorch path's
// arithmetic, in float32.
//
// Layout. Volumes are stored with a border of one zero voxel on every side, in
// bricks of 4 x 4 x 4 voxels. Every sample lies inside the volume's box, so its
// eight voxels lie inside the border and read zero where the volume ends: zero
// padding with no bounds checks. The bricks keep the eight voxels in one or two
// 256 byte blocks per channel however the ray crosses the volume, where rows of a
// [Z, Y, X] array would spread them over four lines.
//
// The forward and the geometry gradient read batches packed, up to four volumes
// interleaved as channels, so one vector load serves the whole group and every ray
// is set up once for it; packed projections are [rays, C]. The forward writes
// unpacked projections, [B, ...] with `plane` elements per item.
//
// `box` holds the volume centre (x, y, z), the half extent, the inverse voxel
// spacing, and (size + 1) / 2, the stored coordinate of the centre: twelve floats.
//
// Exactness. The adjoint scatters every sample's trilinear weights from the same
// code that the forward gathers them with, so the two are each other's transpose
// up to the order of float32 additions.

template <int C>
__device__ __forceinline__ void fetch(const float* __restrict__ base, unsigned index, float (&out)[C])
{
    if constexpr (C == 1) {
        out[0] = __ldg(base + index);
    } else if constexpr (C == 2) {
        const float2 t = __ldg(reinterpret_cast<const float2*>(base) + index);
        out[0] = t.x;
        out[1] = t.y;
    } else {
        const float4 t = __ldg(reinterpret_cast<const float4*>(base) + index);
        out[0] = t.x;
        out[1] = t.y;
        out[2] = t.z;
        out[3] = t.w;
    }
}

struct ConeRay {
    // Sample 0 and the step between samples, in stored voxel coordinates.
    float px, py, pz, sx, sy, sz;
    // Step length; the integral is its product with the sum of the samples.
    float weight;
    // Entry and exit along the ray from the source, in the ray's length unit, the
    // axes whose faces set them (-1 for the source or the detector), and the
    // source-to-detector vector and length, for the geometry gradient.
    float entry, exit, length, ex, ey, ez;
    int entry_axis, exit_axis;
};

__device__ __forceinline__ void clip_axis(float origin, float direction, float half, int axis, float& entry,
                                          float& exit, int& entry_axis, int& exit_axis, bool& missed)
{
    if (fabsf(direction) > 1.1920929e-7f) {
        const float t0 = (-half - origin) / direction, t1 = (half - origin) / direction;
        const float lower = fminf(t0, t1), upper = fmaxf(t0, t1);
        if (lower > entry) {
            entry = lower;
            entry_axis = axis;
        }
        if (upper < exit) {
            exit = upper;
            exit_axis = axis;
        }
    } else if (fabsf(origin) > half) {
        missed = true;
    }
}

__device__ __forceinline__ bool cone_ray(const float* __restrict__ source, const float* __restrict__ detector,
                                         const float* __restrict__ box, int n, ConeRay& ray)
{
    const float sx = source[0], sy = source[1], sz = source[2];
    ray.ex = detector[0] - sx;
    ray.ey = detector[1] - sy;
    ray.ez = detector[2] - sz;
    ray.length = sqrtf(ray.ex * ray.ex + ray.ey * ray.ey + ray.ez * ray.ez);
    if (!(ray.length > 0.f)) return false;
    const float ux = ray.ex / ray.length, uy = ray.ey / ray.length, uz = ray.ez / ray.length;
    const float ox = sx - box[0], oy = sy - box[1], oz = sz - box[2];
    ray.entry = 0.f;
    ray.exit = ray.length;
    ray.entry_axis = ray.exit_axis = -1;
    bool missed = false;
    clip_axis(ox, ux, box[3], 0, ray.entry, ray.exit, ray.entry_axis, ray.exit_axis, missed);
    clip_axis(oy, uy, box[4], 1, ray.entry, ray.exit, ray.entry_axis, ray.exit_axis, missed);
    clip_axis(oz, uz, box[5], 2, ray.entry, ray.exit, ray.entry_axis, ray.exit_axis, missed);
    const float span = ray.exit - ray.entry;
    if (missed || !(span > 0.f)) return false;
    ray.weight = span / (float)n;
    const float first = ray.entry + 0.5f * ray.weight;
    ray.px = __fmaf_rn(first, ux, ox) * box[6] + box[9];
    ray.py = __fmaf_rn(first, uy, oy) * box[7] + box[10];
    ray.pz = __fmaf_rn(first, uz, oz) * box[8] + box[11];
    ray.sx = ray.weight * ux * box[6];
    ray.sy = ray.weight * uy * box[7];
    ray.sz = ray.weight * uz * box[8];
    return true;
}

// The stored volume: X, Y, Z voxels plus the border, in bricks.
struct Layout {
    int X, Y, Z;
    unsigned bricks_x, row, plane;
};

__device__ __forceinline__ Layout layout(int X, int Y, int Z)
{
    Layout l;
    l.X = X;
    l.Y = Y;
    l.Z = Z;
    l.bricks_x = (unsigned)(X + 5) >> 2;
    l.row = l.bricks_x << 6;
    l.plane = l.row * ((unsigned)(Y + 5) >> 2);
    return l;
}

// The eight voxels around a sample: the stored index of the lowest, the steps to
// its neighbours along x, y and z, which cross into the next brick from the last
// voxel of one, and the trilinear weights.
struct Stencil {
    int x0, y0, z0;
    unsigned index, ox, oy, oz;
    float weight[8];
    float dx, dy, dz;
};

__device__ __forceinline__ unsigned corner(const Stencil& s, int q)
{
    return s.index + ((q & 1) ? s.ox : 0u) + ((q & 2) ? s.oy : 0u) + ((q & 4) ? s.oz : 0u);
}

// The stored index of voxel (x, y, z) and the steps to its neighbours along x, y
// and z, which cross into the next brick from the last voxel of one. A sample inside
// the box has 0 <= floor <= size; the clamp only guards memory.
__device__ __forceinline__ void cell(int x0, int y0, int z0, const Layout& l, unsigned& index, unsigned& ox,
                                     unsigned& oy, unsigned& oz)
{
    const unsigned x = min(max(x0, 0), l.X), y = min(max(y0, 0), l.Y), z = min(max(z0, 0), l.Z);
    index = ((z >> 2) * (l.plane >> 6) + (y >> 2) * l.bricks_x + (x >> 2)) << 6 | (z & 3) << 4 | (y & 3) << 2 |
            (x & 3);
    ox = (x & 3) == 3 ? 61u : 1u;
    oy = (y & 3) == 3 ? l.row - 12u : 4u;
    oz = (z & 3) == 3 ? l.plane - 48u : 16u;
}

__device__ __forceinline__ void stencil(float px, float py, float pz, const Layout& l, Stencil& s)
{
    s.x0 = __float2int_rd(px);
    s.y0 = __float2int_rd(py);
    s.z0 = __float2int_rd(pz);
    s.dx = px - (float)s.x0;
    s.dy = py - (float)s.y0;
    s.dz = pz - (float)s.z0;
    cell(s.x0, s.y0, s.z0, l, s.index, s.ox, s.oy, s.oz);
    const float wx[2] = {1.f - s.dx, s.dx}, wy[2] = {1.f - s.dy, s.dy}, wz[2] = {1.f - s.dz, s.dz};
#pragma unroll
    for (int q = 0; q < 8; ++q) s.weight[q] = (wz[q >> 2] * wy[(q >> 1) & 1]) * wx[q & 1];
}

__device__ __forceinline__ bool ray_coordinates(int R, int W, size_t& ray, int& view)
{
    const int column = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
    const int row = blockIdx.y * blockDim.y + threadIdx.y;
    view = blockIdx.z;
    ray = ((size_t)view * R + row) * W + column;
    return column < W && row < R;
}

__device__ __forceinline__ void sample(const ConeRay& ray, int k, const Layout& l, Stencil& s)
{
    const float kf = (float)k;
    stencil(__fmaf_rn(kf, ray.sx, ray.px), __fmaf_rn(kf, ray.sy, ray.py), __fmaf_rn(kf, ray.sz, ray.pz), l, s);
}

// One thread per ray, an 8 x 16 patch of detector elements per block, so a warp's
// loads at each sample land in a compact patch of the volume. The loop is bound by
// its instructions, not by memory: consecutive samples a fraction of a voxel apart
// often share all eight voxels, and then neither their indices nor their values are
// computed or loaded again.
template <int C>
__device__ void cone_forward(const float* __restrict__ volume, const float* __restrict__ sources,
                             const float* __restrict__ detectors, const float* __restrict__ box,
                             float* __restrict__ out, int X, int Y, int Z, int R, int W, int n, int nb,
                             size_t plane)
{
    size_t r;
    int view;
    if (!ray_coordinates(R, W, r, view)) return;
    const Layout l = layout(X, Y, Z);
    float acc[C];
#pragma unroll
    for (int c = 0; c < C; ++c) acc[c] = 0.f;
    float weight = 0.f;
    ConeRay ray;
    if (cone_ray(sources + 3 * view, detectors + 3 * r, box, n, ray)) {
        weight = ray.weight;
        float v[8][C];
        int x0 = -8, y0 = -8, z0 = -8;
        for (int k = 0; k < n; ++k) {
            const float kf = (float)k;
            const float px = __fmaf_rn(kf, ray.sx, ray.px), py = __fmaf_rn(kf, ray.sy, ray.py),
                        pz = __fmaf_rn(kf, ray.sz, ray.pz);
            const int ix = __float2int_rd(px), iy = __float2int_rd(py), iz = __float2int_rd(pz);
            if (ix != x0 || iy != y0 || iz != z0) {
                x0 = ix;
                y0 = iy;
                z0 = iz;
                unsigned index, ox, oy, oz;
                cell(ix, iy, iz, l, index, ox, oy, oz);
#pragma unroll
                for (int q = 0; q < 8; ++q)
                    fetch<C>(volume, index + ((q & 1) ? ox : 0u) + ((q & 2) ? oy : 0u) + ((q & 4) ? oz : 0u), v[q]);
            }
            const float dx = px - (float)ix, dy = py - (float)iy, dz = pz - (float)iz;
            if constexpr (C == 1) {
                const float a = __fmaf_rn(dx, v[1][0] - v[0][0], v[0][0]);
                const float b = __fmaf_rn(dx, v[3][0] - v[2][0], v[2][0]);
                const float c = __fmaf_rn(dx, v[5][0] - v[4][0], v[4][0]);
                const float d = __fmaf_rn(dx, v[7][0] - v[6][0], v[6][0]);
                const float lower = __fmaf_rn(dy, b - a, a), upper = __fmaf_rn(dy, d - c, c);
                acc[0] += __fmaf_rn(dz, upper - lower, lower);
            } else {
                const float wx[2] = {1.f - dx, dx}, wy[2] = {1.f - dy, dy}, wz[2] = {1.f - dz, dz};
#pragma unroll
                for (int q = 0; q < 8; ++q) {
                    const float w = (wz[q >> 2] * wy[(q >> 1) & 1]) * wx[q & 1];
#pragma unroll
                    for (int c = 0; c < C; ++c) acc[c] = __fmaf_rn(w, v[q][c], acc[c]);
                }
            }
        }
    }
#pragma unroll
    for (int c = 0; c < C; ++c)
        if (c < nb) out[(size_t)c * plane + r] = acc[c] * weight;
}

// The transpose, for one volume: each ray scatters its value, times the step
// length, into the voxels its samples read. Atomics, not the samples, bound it, so
// a ray sums its consecutive samples' weights for each voxel in registers and
// scatters a voxel once its stencil has moved past it. A stencil that moves at most
// one voxel along each axis keeps the voxels it shares with the last one. Packing
// volumes as channels would not help: the atomics are per channel.
__device__ void cone_adjoint(const float* __restrict__ projections, const float* __restrict__ sources,
                             const float* __restrict__ detectors, const float* __restrict__ box,
                             float* __restrict__ out, int X, int Y, int Z, int R, int W, int n)
{
    size_t r;
    int view;
    if (!ray_coordinates(R, W, r, view)) return;
    float value = projections[r];
    ConeRay ray;
    if (value == 0.f || !cone_ray(sources + 3 * view, detectors + 3 * r, box, n, ray)) return;
    value *= ray.weight;
    const Layout l = layout(X, Y, Z);
    float pending[8];
    unsigned pending_index[8];
#pragma unroll
    for (int q = 0; q < 8; ++q) {
        pending[q] = 0.f;
        pending_index[q] = 0;
    }
    int x0 = -8, y0 = -8, z0 = -8;
    for (int k = 0; k <= n; ++k) {
        Stencil s;
        if (k < n) {
            sample(ray, k, l, s);
        } else {
            s.x0 = s.y0 = s.z0 = -16;
        }
        const int dx = x0 - s.x0, dy = y0 - s.y0, dz = z0 - s.z0;
        if (dx != 0 || dy != 0 || dz != 0) {
            const bool near = abs(dx) <= 1 && abs(dy) <= 1 && abs(dz) <= 1;
            float kept[8];
#pragma unroll
            for (int q = 0; q < 8; ++q) kept[q] = 0.f;
#pragma unroll
            for (int q = 0; q < 8; ++q) {
                const int i = (q & 1) + dx, j = ((q >> 1) & 1) + dy, h = (q >> 2) + dz;
                if (near && i >= 0 && i <= 1 && j >= 0 && j <= 1 && h >= 0 && h <= 1) {
                    const int target = i + 2 * j + 4 * h;
#pragma unroll
                    for (int u = 0; u < 8; ++u)
                        if (u == target) kept[u] += pending[q];
                } else if (pending[q] != 0.f) {
                    atomicAdd(out + pending_index[q], pending[q]);
                }
            }
            x0 = s.x0;
            y0 = s.y0;
            z0 = s.z0;
#pragma unroll
            for (int q = 0; q < 8; ++q) {
                pending[q] = kept[q];
                pending_index[q] = corner(s, q);
            }
        }
        if (k < n) {
#pragma unroll
            for (int q = 0; q < 8; ++q) pending[q] = __fmaf_rn(s.weight[q], value, pending[q]);
        }
    }
}

// d<g, A x> / d(source) and / d(detector element), summed over the batch. With
// P_k = S + tau_k E, E = D - S, l = |E|, and tau_k running from the entry to the exit
// fraction in n equal steps, the projection is l (tau_x - tau_e) / n sum_k f(P_k).
// Its derivative needs, per ray, F = sum_k f, G = sum_k grad f, and
// M = sum_k (k + 1/2) / n grad f; an entry or exit on a face of axis a, at fraction
// tau, moves with dtau = -((1 - tau) dS_a + tau dD_a) / E_a. f is g . volume, the
// batch weighted by the incoming gradient of this ray.
template <int C>
__device__ void cone_geometry_grad(const float* __restrict__ volume, const float* __restrict__ grad,
                                   const float* __restrict__ sources, const float* __restrict__ detectors,
                                   const float* __restrict__ box, float* __restrict__ grad_sources,
                                   float* __restrict__ grad_detectors, int X, int Y, int Z, int R, int W, int n)
{
    size_t r;
    int view;
    const bool active = ray_coordinates(R, W, r, view);
    float gs[3] = {0.f, 0.f, 0.f}, gd[3] = {0.f, 0.f, 0.f};
    float g[C];
    bool any = false;
    if (active) {
        fetch<C>(grad, (unsigned)r, g);
#pragma unroll
        for (int c = 0; c < C; ++c) any = any || g[c] != 0.f;
    }
    ConeRay ray;
    if (any && cone_ray(sources + 3 * view, detectors + 3 * r, box, n, ray)) {
        const Layout l = layout(X, Y, Z);
        float F = 0.f, G[3] = {0.f, 0.f, 0.f}, M[3] = {0.f, 0.f, 0.f};
        float v[8];
        unsigned loaded = 0xffffffffu;
        for (int k = 0; k < n; ++k) {
            Stencil s;
            sample(ray, k, l, s);
            if (s.index != loaded) {
#pragma unroll
                for (int q = 0; q < 8; ++q) {
                    float value[C];
                    fetch<C>(volume, corner(s, q), value);
                    float weighted = 0.f;
#pragma unroll
                    for (int c = 0; c < C; ++c) weighted = __fmaf_rn(g[c], value[c], weighted);
                    v[q] = weighted;
                }
                loaded = s.index;
            }
            const float ax = 1.f - s.dx, ay = 1.f - s.dy, az = 1.f - s.dz;
            const float f = az * (ay * (ax * v[0] + s.dx * v[1]) + s.dy * (ax * v[2] + s.dx * v[3])) +
                            s.dz * (ay * (ax * v[4] + s.dx * v[5]) + s.dy * (ax * v[6] + s.dx * v[7]));
            const float fx = az * (ay * (v[1] - v[0]) + s.dy * (v[3] - v[2])) +
                             s.dz * (ay * (v[5] - v[4]) + s.dy * (v[7] - v[6]));
            const float fy = az * (ax * (v[2] - v[0]) + s.dx * (v[3] - v[1])) +
                             s.dz * (ax * (v[6] - v[4]) + s.dx * (v[7] - v[5]));
            const float fz = ay * (ax * (v[4] - v[0]) + s.dx * (v[5] - v[1])) +
                             s.dy * (ax * (v[6] - v[2]) + s.dx * (v[7] - v[3]));
            const float w = ((float)k + 0.5f) / (float)n;
            F += f;
            G[0] += fx;
            G[1] += fy;
            G[2] += fz;
            M[0] = __fmaf_rn(w, fx, M[0]);
            M[1] = __fmaf_rn(w, fy, M[1]);
            M[2] = __fmaf_rn(w, fz, M[2]);
        }
        const float E[3] = {ray.ex, ray.ey, ray.ez};
        const float length = ray.length;
        const float tau_e = ray.entry / length, tau_x = ray.exit / length, delta = tau_x - tau_e;
        const float step = ray.weight;  // length * delta / n
        float GE = 0.f, ME = 0.f;
#pragma unroll
        for (int a = 0; a < 3; ++a) {
            G[a] *= box[6 + a];
            M[a] *= box[6 + a];
            GE = __fmaf_rn(G[a], E[a], GE);
            ME = __fmaf_rn(M[a], E[a], ME);
        }
        const float along = delta * F / (float)n;
        const float ends = length * F / (float)n;
#pragma unroll
        for (int a = 0; a < 3; ++a) {
            const float H = tau_e * G[a] + delta * M[a];
            gd[a] = along * E[a] / length + step * H;
            gs[a] = step * G[a] - gd[a];
        }
#pragma unroll
        for (int a = 0; a < 3; ++a) {
            if (a == ray.exit_axis) {
                const float c = (ends + step * ME) / E[a];
                gs[a] -= c * (1.f - tau_x);
                gd[a] -= c * tau_x;
            }
            if (a == ray.entry_axis) {
                const float c = (-ends + step * (GE - ME)) / E[a];
                gs[a] -= c * (1.f - tau_e);
                gd[a] -= c * tau_e;
            }
        }
    }
    if (active) {
#pragma unroll
        for (int a = 0; a < 3; ++a) grad_detectors[3 * r + a] = gd[a];
    }
    // Every ray of a block belongs to one view.
#pragma unroll
    for (int a = 0; a < 3; ++a) {
        float total = gs[a];
#pragma unroll
        for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) total += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, total, offset);
        if ((threadIdx.x + blockDim.x * threadIdx.y) % 32 == 0 && total != 0.f)
            atomicAdd(grad_sources + 3 * view + a, total);
    }
}

#define TT_CONE(C)                                                                                            \
    extern "C" __global__ void __launch_bounds__(128)                                                         \
        cone_forward_c##C(const float* volume, const float* sources, const float* detectors, const float* box, \
                          float* out, int X, int Y, int Z, int R, int W, int n, int nb, size_t plane)          \
    {                                                                                                         \
        cone_forward<C>(volume, sources, detectors, box, out, X, Y, Z, R, W, n, nb, plane);                   \
    }                                                                                                         \
    extern "C" __global__ void __launch_bounds__(128) cone_geometry_grad_c##C(                                \
        const float* volume, const float* grad, const float* sources, const float* detectors, const float* box, \
        float* grad_sources, float* grad_detectors, int X, int Y, int Z, int R, int W, int n)                  \
    {                                                                                                         \
        cone_geometry_grad<C>(volume, grad, sources, detectors, box, grad_sources, grad_detectors, X, Y, Z, R, \
                              W, n);                                                                          \
    }

TT_CONE(1)
TT_CONE(2)
TT_CONE(4)

extern "C" __global__ void __launch_bounds__(128)
    cone_adjoint_c1(const float* projections, const float* sources, const float* detectors, const float* box,
                    float* out, int X, int Y, int Z, int R, int W, int n)
{
    cone_adjoint(projections, sources, detectors, box, out, X, Y, Z, R, W, n);
}

// FDK backprojection. A view's matrix, twelve floats, maps a voxel index (x, y, z, 1)
// to (column w, row w, w), w being the voxel's depth along the panel normal over the
// panel's. A voxel gathers every view's filtered projection there, bilinear with zero
// off the panel, weighted by 1 / w^2. The transpose scatters the same weights, from
// the same code, so the two are each other's transpose up to float32 addition order.

struct PanelStencil {
    unsigned index[4];
    float weight[4];
};

// A view's matrix rows with the voxel's x and y applied: what is left is linear in z.
struct ViewColumn {
    float column, column_z, row, row_z, depth, depth_z;
};

__device__ __forceinline__ ViewColumn view_column(const float* __restrict__ matrix, float x, float y)
{
    const float4 c = __ldg(reinterpret_cast<const float4*>(matrix));
    const float4 r = __ldg(reinterpret_cast<const float4*>(matrix) + 1);
    const float4 d = __ldg(reinterpret_cast<const float4*>(matrix) + 2);
    return {fmaf(c.x, x, fmaf(c.y, y, c.w)), c.z, fmaf(r.x, x, fmaf(r.y, y, r.w)), r.z,
            fmaf(d.x, x, fmaf(d.y, y, d.w)), d.z};
}

__device__ __forceinline__ bool panel_stencil(const ViewColumn& v, float z, int rows, int columns, PanelStencil& s)
{
    const float w = fmaf(v.depth_z, z, v.depth);
    if (!(w > 0.f)) return false;
    const float inverse = 1.f / w;
    const float column = fmaf(v.column_z, z, v.column) * inverse;
    const float row = fmaf(v.row_z, z, v.row) * inverse;
    if (!(column > -1.f && column < (float)columns && row > -1.f && row < (float)rows)) return false;
    const float column_floor = floorf(column), row_floor = floorf(row);
    const int c0 = (int)column_floor, r0 = (int)row_floor;
    const float dc = column - column_floor, dr = row - row_floor;
    const float scale = inverse * inverse;
    const float wc0 = c0 >= 0 ? 1.f - dc : 0.f;
    const float wc1 = c0 + 1 < columns ? dc : 0.f;
    const float wr0 = r0 >= 0 ? (1.f - dr) * scale : 0.f;
    const float wr1 = r0 + 1 < rows ? dr * scale : 0.f;
    const unsigned left = max(c0, 0), right = min(c0 + 1, columns - 1);
    const unsigned top = max(r0, 0) * (unsigned)columns, bottom = min(r0 + 1, rows - 1) * (unsigned)columns;
    s.index[0] = top + left;
    s.index[1] = top + right;
    s.index[2] = bottom + left;
    s.index[3] = bottom + right;
    s.weight[0] = wr0 * wc0;
    s.weight[1] = wr0 * wc1;
    s.weight[2] = wr1 * wc0;
    s.weight[3] = wr1 * wc1;
    return true;
}

// Each thread backprojects TT_FDK_DEPTH voxels along z, sharing its view loads.
#define TT_FDK_DEPTH 4

template <int C>
__device__ __forceinline__ void fdk_backproject(const float* __restrict__ projections,
                                                const float* __restrict__ matrices, float* __restrict__ out, int X,
                                                int Y, int Z, int views, int rows, int columns, int nb, size_t plane)
{
    const int x = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
    const int y = blockIdx.y * blockDim.y + threadIdx.y;
    const int z0 = blockIdx.z * TT_FDK_DEPTH;
    if (x >= X || y >= Y) return;
    const size_t panel = (size_t)rows * columns;
    float acc[TT_FDK_DEPTH][C] = {};
    for (int view = 0; view < views; ++view) {
        const ViewColumn v = view_column(matrices + 12 * view, (float)x, (float)y);
        const float* base = projections + view * panel * C;
#pragma unroll
        for (int j = 0; j < TT_FDK_DEPTH; ++j) {
            PanelStencil s;
            if (z0 + j >= Z || !panel_stencil(v, (float)(z0 + j), rows, columns, s)) continue;
#pragma unroll
            for (int q = 0; q < 4; ++q) {
                float p[C];
                fetch<C>(base, s.index[q], p);
#pragma unroll
                for (int k = 0; k < C; ++k) acc[j][k] = fmaf(s.weight[q], p[k], acc[j][k]);
            }
        }
    }
#pragma unroll
    for (int j = 0; j < TT_FDK_DEPTH; ++j) {
        if (z0 + j >= Z) break;
        const size_t voxel = ((size_t)(z0 + j) * Y + y) * X + x;
#pragma unroll
        for (int k = 0; k < C; ++k)
            if (k < nb) out[k * plane + voxel] = acc[j][k];
    }
}

#define TT_FDK(C)                                                                                             \
    extern "C" __global__ void __launch_bounds__(128)                                                         \
        fdk_backproject_c##C(const float* projections, const float* matrices, float* out, int X, int Y, int Z, \
                             int views, int rows, int columns, int nb, size_t plane)                           \
    {                                                                                                         \
        fdk_backproject<C>(projections, matrices, out, X, Y, Z, views, rows, columns, nb, plane);             \
    }

TT_FDK(1)
TT_FDK(2)
TT_FDK(4)

extern "C" __global__ void __launch_bounds__(128)
    fdk_backproject_transpose_c1(const float* volume, const float* matrices, float* out, int X, int Y, int Z,
                                 int views, int rows, int columns)
{
    const int x = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
    const int y = blockIdx.y * blockDim.y + threadIdx.y;
    const int z0 = blockIdx.z * TT_FDK_DEPTH;
    if (x >= X || y >= Y) return;
    float value[TT_FDK_DEPTH];
    bool any = false;
#pragma unroll
    for (int j = 0; j < TT_FDK_DEPTH; ++j) {
        value[j] = z0 + j < Z ? volume[((size_t)(z0 + j) * Y + y) * X + x] : 0.f;
        any |= value[j] != 0.f;
    }
    if (!any) return;
    const size_t panel = (size_t)rows * columns;
    for (int view = 0; view < views; ++view) {
        const ViewColumn v = view_column(matrices + 12 * view, (float)x, (float)y);
        float* base = out + view * panel;
#pragma unroll
        for (int j = 0; j < TT_FDK_DEPTH; ++j) {
            PanelStencil s;
            if (value[j] == 0.f || !panel_stencil(v, (float)(z0 + j), rows, columns, s)) continue;
#pragma unroll
            for (int q = 0; q < 4; ++q)
                if (s.weight[q] != 0.f) atomicAdd(base + s.index[q], s.weight[q] * value[j]);
        }
    }
}
```

### src/torchtomo/_cone_kernels.py
```
"""Python side of the runtime-compiled cone beam kernels in _cone_kernels.cu.

The kernels read volumes in their own layout: a border of one zero voxel on every
side, in bricks of 4 x 4 x 4 voxels. The forward and the geometry gradient read up
to four volumes of the batch interleaved as channels, so one vector load serves the
group and every ray is set up once for it; the adjoint scatters one volume at a
time. Converting to and from the layout is one copy of the volume.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import ctypes
import math
import os

import torch

from ._cuda_kernels import _groups, _pack
from ._nvrtc import KernelLibrary

_SOURCE = "_cone_kernels.cu"
_BLOCK = (8, 16, 1)
_GROUP = 4
_BRICK = 4

_library: KernelLibrary | None = None


def _kernels() -> KernelLibrary:
    global _library
    if _library is None:
        with open(os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), _SOURCE), encoding="utf-8") as handle:
            _library = KernelLibrary(handle.read(), "torchtomo_cone.cu")
    return _library


def volume_box(volume_shape, spacing_xyz: torch.Tensor, center: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
    """The kernels' twelve floats: centre, half extent, inverse spacing, and stored centre, each (x, y, z)."""
    size_xyz = torch.tensor(volume_shape[::-1], dtype=torch.float64, device=spacing_xyz.device)
    spacing = spacing_xyz.detach().double()
    values = [center.detach().double(), size_xyz * spacing / 2, 1 / spacing, (size_xyz + 1) / 2]
    return torch.cat(values).to(torch.float32).contiguous()


def kernels_fit(volume_shape, projection_shape) -> bool:
    """The kernels index the stored volume and the projections with 32 bits."""
    return _stored_size(volume_shape) < 2**32 and math.prod(projection_shape) < 2**32


def _bricks(volume_shape) -> tuple[int, int, int]:
    return tuple(-(-(size + 2) // _BRICK) for size in volume_shape)


def _stored_size(volume_shape) -> int:
    return math.prod(_bricks(volume_shape)) * _BRICK**3


def _lattice(stored: torch.Tensor, volume_shape) -> torch.Tensor:
    """A [bricks * 64, group] stored volume as [group, Z, Y, X], the border cropped."""
    depth, height, width = volume_shape
    bz, by, bx = _bricks(volume_shape)
    b = _BRICK
    lattice = stored.view(bz, by, bx, b, b, b, -1).permute(6, 0, 3, 1, 4, 2, 5)
    return lattice.reshape(-1, b * bz, b * by, b * bx)[:, 1 : depth + 1, 1 : height + 1, 1 : width + 1]


def _brick(volume: torch.Tensor, start: int, count: int, group: int) -> torch.Tensor:
    """Volumes start..start+count of [B, 1, Z, Y, X] in the kernels' layout, [bricks * 64, group]."""
    depth, height, width = volume.shape[2:]
    bz, by, bx = _bricks((depth, height, width))
    b = _BRICK
    padded = volume.new_zeros(count, b * bz, b * by, b * bx)
    padded[:, 1 : depth + 1, 1 : height + 1, 1 : width + 1] = volume[start : start + count, 0]
    bricks = padded.view(count, bz, b, by, b, bx, b).permute(1, 3, 5, 2, 4, 6, 0)
    if count == group:
        return bricks.reshape(-1, group)
    stored = volume.new_zeros(bz, by, bx, b, b, b, group)
    stored[..., :count] = bricks
    return stored.view(-1, group)


def _grid(projection_shape) -> tuple[int, int, int]:
    views, rows, columns = projection_shape
    return (math.ceil(columns / _BLOCK[0]), math.ceil(rows / _BLOCK[1]), views)


def _geometry(sources: torch.Tensor, detectors: torch.Tensor) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    return sources.detach().float().contiguous(), detectors.detach().float().contiguous()


def cone_forward(
    volume: torch.Tensor, sources: torch.Tensor, detectors: torch.Tensor, box: torch.Tensor, n_samples: int
) -> torch.Tensor:
    """[B, 1, Z, Y, X] -> [B, 1, views, rows, columns]."""
    batch, _, depth, height, width = volume.shape
    projection_shape = tuple(detectors.shape[:3])
    plane = math.prod(projection_shape)
    sources, detectors = _geometry(sources, detectors)
    out = volume.new_empty(batch, 1, *projection_shape)
    library = _kernels()
    for start, count, group in _groups(batch, _GROUP):
        stored = _brick(volume, start, count, group)
        kernel = library.function(f"cone_forward_c{group}", volume.device)
        kernel(
            _grid(projection_shape),
            _BLOCK,
            [stored, sources, detectors, box, out[start], width, height, depth, *projection_shape[1:], n_samples]
            + [count, ctypes.c_size_t(plane)],
        )
    return out


def cone_adjoint(
    projections: torch.Tensor,
    sources: torch.Tensor,
    detectors: torch.Tensor,
    box: torch.Tensor,
    n_samples: int,
    volume_shape: tuple[int, int, int],
) -> torch.Tensor:
    """[B, 1, views, rows, columns] -> [B, 1, Z, Y, X], the transpose of cone_forward."""
    batch = projections.shape[0]
    projection_shape = tuple(projections.shape[2:])
    depth, height, width = volume_shape
    sources, detectors = _geometry(sources, detectors)
    flat = projections.reshape(batch, -1)
    out = projections.new_empty(batch, 1, *volume_shape)
    kernel = _kernels().function("cone_adjoint_c1", projections.device)
    for item in range(batch):
        stored = projections.new_zeros(_stored_size(volume_shape), 1)
        kernel(
            _grid(projection_shape),
            _BLOCK,
            [flat[item], sources, detectors, box, stored, width, height, depth, *projection_shape[1:], n_samples],
        )
        out[item, 0] = _lattice(stored, volume_shape)[0]
    return out


def cone_geometry_grad(
    volume: torch.Tensor,
    grad_projections: torch.Tensor,
    sources: torch.Tensor,
    detectors: torch.Tensor,
    box: torch.Tensor,
    n_samples: int,
) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    """d<g, A x> / d(sources) and / d(detectors), [views, 3] and [views, rows, columns, 3], summed over the batch."""
    batch, _, depth, height, width = volume.shape
    projection_shape = tuple(detectors.shape[:3])
    sources, detectors = _geometry(sources, detectors)
    grad_flat = grad_projections.reshape(batch, -1)
    grad_sources = torch.zeros_like(sources)
    grad_detectors = torch.empty_like(detectors)
    library = _kernels()
    for start, count, group in _groups(batch, _GROUP):
        stored = _brick(volume, start, count, group)
        packed_grad = _pack(grad_flat, start, count, group)
        group_detectors = grad_detectors if start == 0 else torch.empty_like(detectors)
        kernel = library.function(f"cone_geometry_grad_c{group}", volume.device)
        kernel(
            _grid(projection_shape),
            _BLOCK,
            [stored, packed_grad, sources, detectors, box, grad_sources, group_detectors, width, height, depth]
            + [*projection_shape[1:], n_samples],
        )
        if start > 0:
            grad_detectors += group_detectors
    return grad_sources, grad_detectors


_VOLUME_BLOCK = (32, 4, 1)
_FDK_DEPTH = 4


def _volume_grid(volume_shape) -> tuple[int, int, int]:
    depth, height, width = volume_shape
    return (math.ceil(width / _VOLUME_BLOCK[0]), math.ceil(height / _VOLUME_BLOCK[1]), math.ceil(depth / _FDK_DEPTH))


def fdk_backproject(projections: torch.Tensor, matrices: torch.Tensor, volume_shape) -> torch.Tensor:
    """Filtered [B, 1, views, rows, columns] -> [B, 1, Z, Y, X], weighted by (D / l)^2; see _fdk."""
    batch = projections.shape[0]
    views, rows, columns = projections.shape[2:]
    depth, height, width = volume_shape
    flat = projections.reshape(batch, -1)
    matrices = matrices.detach().float().contiguous()
    out = projections.new_empty(batch, 1, *volume_shape)
    library = _kernels()
    for start, count, group in _groups(batch, _GROUP):
        kernel = library.function(f"fdk_backproject_c{group}", projections.device)
        kernel(
            _volume_grid(volume_shape),
            _VOLUME_BLOCK,
            [_pack(flat, start, count, group), matrices, out[start], width, height, depth, views, rows, columns]
            + [count, ctypes.c_size_t(depth * height * width)],
        )
    return out


def fdk_backproject_transpose(volume: torch.Tensor, matrices: torch.Tensor, projection_shape) -> torch.Tensor:
    """[B, 1, Z, Y, X] -> [B, 1, views, rows, columns], the transpose of fdk_backproject."""
    batch, _, depth, height, width = volume.shape
    views, rows, columns = projection_shape
    matrices = matrices.detach().float().contiguous()
    out = volume.new_zeros(batch, 1, *projection_shape)
    kernel = _kernels().function("fdk_backproject_transpose_c1", volume.device)
    for item in range(batch):
        kernel(
            _volume_grid((depth, height, width)),
            _VOLUME_BLOCK,
            [volume[item, 0].contiguous(), matrices, out[item, 0], width, height, depth, views, rows, columns],
        )
    return out
```

### src/torchtomo/_cuda_kernels.cu
```
// Projector kernels for torchtomo, compiled at run time by NVRTC (see _nvrtc.py).
//
// Images and sinograms reach these kernels packed: up to eight images of the batch
// interleaved as channels, so one vector load serves every image and the geometry
// (coordinates, bilinear weights) is computed once per sample, not once per image.
// A packed image is [S, S, C] and a packed sinogram [A, n_det, C], C in {1, 2, 4, 8}.
// Outputs are written unpacked, [B, ...] with `plane` elements per image.
//
// Pixel coordinates follow grid_sample with align_corners=True: pixel j sits at
// normalised x = -1 + 2 j / (S - 1), so the centre is c = (S - 1) / 2.
//
// Exactness. The adjoint kernels are the transpose of the forward kernels, not of
// the eager path: both sides compute each sample point with the same fused
// multiply-adds and derive the bilinear weights with the same floor, so every
// weight is bit-identical and <A x, y> = <x, A^T y> holds to float32 roundoff.

#define TT_SQRT2_MARGIN 1.4152f
// Sample windows are widened by this many pixels, far beyond the rounding of a
// sample coordinate (half an ulp: 3e-5 px at 512 px).
#define TT_WINDOW_SLACK 1e-3f

template <int C>
__device__ __forceinline__ void fetch(const float* __restrict__ base, int index, float (&out)[C])
{
    if constexpr (C == 1) {
        out[0] = __ldg(base + index);
    } else if constexpr (C == 2) {
        const float2 t = __ldg(reinterpret_cast<const float2*>(base) + index);
        out[0] = t.x;
        out[1] = t.y;
    } else {
        const float4* p = reinterpret_cast<const float4*>(base) + (size_t)index * (C / 4);
#pragma unroll
        for (int q = 0; q < C / 4; ++q) {
            const float4 t = __ldg(p + q);
            out[4 * q + 0] = t.x;
            out[4 * q + 1] = t.y;
            out[4 * q + 2] = t.z;
            out[4 * q + 3] = t.w;
        }
    }
}

// Bilinear gather at pixel coordinates (px, py) with zero padding, accumulated into acc.
template <int C>
__device__ __forceinline__ void bilinear_add(const float* __restrict__ img, int S, float px, float py, float (&acc)[C])
{
    const float xf = floorf(px), yf = floorf(py);
    const int x0 = (int)xf, y0 = (int)yf;
    const float dx = px - xf, dy = py - yf;
    const float wx0 = (x0 >= 0 && x0 < S) ? 1.f - dx : 0.f;
    const float wx1 = (x0 + 1 >= 0 && x0 + 1 < S) ? dx : 0.f;
    const float wy0 = (y0 >= 0 && y0 < S) ? 1.f - dy : 0.f;
    const float wy1 = (y0 + 1 >= 0 && y0 + 1 < S) ? dy : 0.f;
    if (wx0 == 0.f && wx1 == 0.f) return;
    if (wy0 == 0.f && wy1 == 0.f) return;
    const int cx0 = min(max(x0, 0), S - 1), cx1 = min(max(x0 + 1, 0), S - 1);
    const int cy0 = min(max(y0, 0), S - 1), cy1 = min(max(y0 + 1, 0), S - 1);
    float p00[C], p10[C], p01[C], p11[C];
    fetch<C>(img, cy0 * S + cx0, p00);
    fetch<C>(img, cy0 * S + cx1, p10);
    fetch<C>(img, cy1 * S + cx0, p01);
    fetch<C>(img, cy1 * S + cx1, p11);
    const float w00 = wx0 * wy0, w10 = wx1 * wy0, w01 = wx0 * wy1, w11 = wx1 * wy1;
#pragma unroll
    for (int k = 0; k < C; ++k) acc[k] += w00 * p00[k] + w10 * p10[k] + w01 * p01[k] + w11 * p11[k];
}

// The forward's weight for pixel index `pix` from sample coordinate `p`, bit for bit.
__device__ __forceinline__ float tent_as_forward(float p, float pix)
{
    const float f = floorf(p);
    const float d = p - f;
    return (f == pix) ? 1.f - d : ((f + 1.f == pix) ? d : 0.f);
}

// View tables. A plain pose, where no view is shifted, is (cos, sin) per view as
// float2 and runs exactly the arithmetic that predates shifts: reading a float4
// costs the parallel FBP backprojection 15% even when its shifts are zero. A
// shifted pose is (cos, sin, detector shift, source shift) as float4, in the
// kernel's length unit. The kernels that read one are built for both, and
// `_shifted` in the name picks the float4 table.
template <bool SHIFTED>
struct view_table {
    typedef float2 type;
};
template <>
struct view_table<true> {
    typedef float4 type;
};

__device__ __forceinline__ float4 as_view(float2 t) { return make_float4(t.x, t.y, 0.f, 0.f); }
__device__ __forceinline__ float4 as_view(float4 t) { return t; }

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Parallel beam. Lattice point (h, w) of angle a, with u = w - c, v = h - c, lands at
//   px = cx + cos(a) u + sin(a) v,  py = cy + cos(a) v - sin(a) u
// and the projection sums the samples over h, times the pixel size. (cx, cy) is the
// lattice centre c moved by the view's detector shift s, in pixels: bin w reads the
// ray at detector coordinate u + s.
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

template <bool SHIFTED>
__device__ __forceinline__ void parallel_centre(float4 t, float c, float& cx, float& cy)
{
    if constexpr (SHIFTED) {
        cx = __fmaf_rn(t.x, t.z, c);
        cy = __fmaf_rn(-t.y, t.z, c);
    } else {
        cx = c;
        cy = c;
    }
}

__device__ __forceinline__ void parallel_point(float cs, float sn, float cx, float cy, float u, float v, float& px,
                                               float& py)
{
    px = __fmaf_rn(sn, v, __fmaf_rn(cs, u, cx));
    py = __fmaf_rn(-sn, u, __fmaf_rn(cs, v, cy));
}

// One warp owns TW detector bins of one angle; its lanes cover a TW x (32 / TW) patch
// of the lattice and step down the rays together, so a warp's loads land in a
// compact rotated patch of the image instead of a 32 pixel line.
template <int C, int TW, bool SHIFTED>
__device__ void parallel_forward(const float* __restrict__ img,
                                 const typename view_table<SHIFTED>::type* __restrict__ pose,
                                 float* __restrict__ out, int S, int A, int nb, int plane, float scale, float r2)
{
    constexpr int TH = 32 / TW;
    const int lane = threadIdx.x & 31, warp = threadIdx.x >> 5;
    const int lw = lane % TW, lh = lane / TW;
    const int a = blockIdx.y;
    const int w_first = (blockIdx.x * (blockDim.x >> 5) + warp) * TW;
    if (w_first >= S) return;
    const int w = w_first + lw;
    const float c = 0.5f * (float)(S - 1);
    const float4 t = as_view(pose[a]);
    const float cs = t.x, sn = t.y;
    float cx, cy;
    parallel_centre<SHIFTED>(t, c, cx, cy);
    const float u = (float)w - c;

    // Rays are bounded by the disc that can still touch the masked image; the bound
    // is the warp's widest ray so all lanes take the same number of steps. A ray's
    // distance from the axis is its detector coordinate, u + s.
    const float u_lo = (float)w_first - c + t.z, u_hi = (float)min(w_first + TW - 1, S - 1) - c + t.z;
    const float u_min = (u_lo <= 0.f && u_hi >= 0.f) ? 0.f : fminf(fabsf(u_lo), fabsf(u_hi));
    int h0 = 0, h1 = S - 1;
    const float room = r2 - u_min * u_min;
    if (room < 0.f) {
        h1 = -1;
    } else if (room < c * c * 4.f) {
        const float e = sqrtf(room);
        h0 = max(0, (int)floorf(c - e));
        h1 = min(S - 1, (int)ceilf(c + e));
    }

    float acc[C];
#pragma unroll
    for (int k = 0; k < C; ++k) acc[k] = 0.f;
    if (w < S) {
        for (int h = h0 + lh; h <= h1; h += TH) {
            float px, py;
            parallel_point(cs, sn, cx, cy, u, (float)h - c, px, py);
            bilinear_add<C>(img, S, px, py, acc);
        }
    }
#pragma unroll
    for (int offset = TW; offset < 32; offset <<= 1) {
#pragma unroll
        for (int k = 0; k < C; ++k) acc[k] += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffffu, acc[k], offset);
    }
    if (lh == 0 && w < S) {
        float* o = out + (size_t)a * S + w;
#pragma unroll
        for (int k = 0; k < C; ++k)
            if (k < nb) o[(size_t)k * plane] = acc[k] * scale;
    }
}

// Gather form of the transpose: each pixel collects from the lattice points whose
// bilinear footprint covers it. Those lie within sqrt(2) of the pixel's rotated
// position in both lattice directions, so three columns and three rows suffice.
template <int C, bool SHIFTED>
__device__ void parallel_adjoint(const float* __restrict__ sino,
                                 const typename view_table<SHIFTED>::type* __restrict__ pose,
                                 const float* __restrict__ mask, float* __restrict__ out, int S, int A, int nb,
                                 int plane, float scale)
{
    const int j = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
    const int i = blockIdx.y * blockDim.y + threadIdx.y;
    if (i >= S || j >= S) return;
    const float m = mask ? mask[i * S + j] : 1.f;
    float acc[C];
#pragma unroll
    for (int k = 0; k < C; ++k) acc[k] = 0.f;
    if (m != 0.f) {
        const float c = 0.5f * (float)(S - 1);
        const float jf = (float)j, iff = (float)i;
        const float X0 = jf - c, Y0 = iff - c;
        for (int a = 0; a < A; ++a) {
            const float4 t = as_view(pose[a]);
            const float cs = t.x, sn = t.y;
            float cx, cy;
            parallel_centre<SHIFTED>(t, c, cx, cy);
            const float X = SHIFTED ? jf - cx : X0, Y = SHIFTED ? iff - cy : Y0;
            const float us = cs * X - sn * Y + c;
            const float vs = sn * X + cs * Y + c;
            const int wb = (int)floorf(us - TT_SQRT2_MARGIN) + 1;
            const int hb = (int)floorf(vs - TT_SQRT2_MARGIN) + 1;
            const float* row = sino + (size_t)a * S * C;
#pragma unroll
            for (int dw = 0; dw < 3; ++dw) {
                const int w = wb + dw;
                if (w < 0 || w >= S) continue;
                const float u = (float)w - c;
                float kw = 0.f;
#pragma unroll
                for (int dh = 0; dh < 3; ++dh) {
                    const int h = hb + dh;
                    if (h < 0 || h >= S) continue;
                    float px, py;
                    parallel_point(cs, sn, cx, cy, u, (float)h - c, px, py);
                    kw += tent_as_forward(px, jf) * tent_as_forward(py, iff);
                }
                if (kw != 0.f) {
                    float y[C];
                    fetch<C>(row, w, y);
#pragma unroll
                    for (int k = 0; k < C; ++k) acc[k] += kw * y[k];
                }
            }
        }
    }
    float* o = out + (size_t)i * S + j;
#pragma unroll
    for (int k = 0; k < C; ++k)
        if (k < nb) o[(size_t)k * plane] = acc[k] * scale * m;
}

// FBP backprojection: pixel-driven, linear interpolation on the detector.
template <int C, bool SHIFTED>
__device__ void parallel_backproject(const float* __restrict__ sino,
                                     const typename view_table<SHIFTED>::type* __restrict__ pose,
                                     const float* __restrict__ coords, const float* __restrict__ mask,
                                     float* __restrict__ out, int S, int A, int n_det, int nb, int plane, float scale)
{
    const int j = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
    const int i = blockIdx.y * blockDim.y + threadIdx.y;
    if (i >= S || j >= S) return;
    const float m = mask ? mask[i * S + j] : 1.f;
    float acc[C];
#pragma unroll
    for (int k = 0; k < C; ++k) acc[k] = 0.f;
    if (m != 0.f) {
        const float nx = coords[j], ny = coords[i];
        const float half = 0.5f * (float)(n_det - 1);
        // A shift of s pixels, each 2 / (S - 1) of the [-1, 1] detector, is this many bins.
        const float bins_per_pixel = (float)(n_det - 1) / (float)max(S - 1, 1);
        for (int a = 0; a < A; ++a) {
            const float4 t = as_view(pose[a]);
            const float g = __fmaf_rn(ny, -t.y, nx * t.x);
            float d = (g + 1.f) * half;
            if constexpr (SHIFTED) d = __fmaf_rn(-t.z, bins_per_pixel, d);
            const float f = floorf(d);
            const int d0 = (int)f;
            const float frac = d - f;
            const float* row = sino + (size_t)a * n_det * C;
            if (d0 >= 0 && d0 < n_det) {
                float y[C];
                fetch<C>(row, d0, y);
#pragma unroll
                for (int k = 0; k < C; ++k) acc[k] += (1.f - frac) * y[k];
            }
            if (d0 + 1 >= 0 && d0 + 1 < n_det) {
                float y[C];
                fetch<C>(row, d0 + 1, y);
#pragma unroll
                for (int k = 0; k < C; ++k) acc[k] += frac * y[k];
            }
        }
    }
    float* o = out + (size_t)i * S + j;
#pragma unroll
    for (int k = 0; k < C; ++k)
        if (k < nb) o[(size_t)k * plane] = acc[k] * scale * m;
}

#define TT_PARALLEL_VARIANT(C, TAG, SHIFTED, TABLE)                                                           \
    extern "C" __global__ void __launch_bounds__(128) parallel_forward##TAG##_c##C(                           \
        const float* img, const TABLE* pose, float* out, int S, int A, int nb, int plane, float scale,        \
        float r2)                                                                                             \
    {                                                                                                         \
        parallel_forward<C, TT_FORWARD_TW, SHIFTED>(img, pose, out, S, A, nb, plane, scale, r2);              \
    }                                                                                                         \
    extern "C" __global__ void parallel_adjoint##TAG##_c##C(const float* sino, const TABLE* pose,             \
                                                            const float* mask, float* out, int S, int A,      \
                                                            int nb, int plane, float scale)                   \
    {                                                                                                         \
        parallel_adjoint<C, SHIFTED>(sino, pose, mask, out, S, A, nb, plane, scale);                          \
    }                                                                                                         \
    extern "C" __global__ void parallel_backproject##TAG##_c##C(const float* sino, const TABLE* pose,         \
                                                                const float* coords, const float* mask,       \
                                                                float* out, int S, int A, int n_det, int nb,  \
                                                                int plane, float scale)                       \
    {                                                                                                         \
        parallel_backproject<C, SHIFTED>(sino, pose, coords, mask, out, S, A, n_det, nb, plane, scale);       \
    }

#define TT_PARALLEL(C)                                                                                        \
    TT_PARALLEL_VARIANT(C, , false, float2)                                                                   \
    TT_PARALLEL_VARIANT(C, _shifted, true, float4)

#ifndef TT_FORWARD_TW
#define TT_FORWARD_TW 4
#endif
#ifndef TT_FAN_ROWS
#define TT_FAN_ROWS 2
#endif

TT_PARALLEL(1)
TT_PARALLEL(2)
TT_PARALLEL(4)
TT_PARALLEL(8)

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Fan beam, flat detector. Every ray (a, d) is a table row in pixel coordinates:
// start (x0, y0) where it enters the unit disc, step (sx, sy) between its n samples,
// and its weight, the chord length over n. Sample k sits at
//   px = x0 + k sx,  py = y0 + k sy
// and the projection is the weight times the sum of the samples.
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

__device__ __forceinline__ void fan_point(const float4 ray, float k, float& px, float& py)
{
    px = __fmaf_rn(k, ray.z, ray.x);
    py = __fmaf_rn(k, ray.w, ray.y);
}

template <int C, int TW>
__device__ void fan_forward(const float* __restrict__ img, const float4* __restrict__ rays,
                            const float* __restrict__ ray_weight, float* __restrict__ out, int S, int A, int n_det,
                            int n_samples, int nb, int plane)
{
    constexpr int TH = 32 / TW;
    const int lane = threadIdx.x & 31, warp = threadIdx.x >> 5;
    const int lw = lane % TW, lh = lane / TW;
    const int a = blockIdx.y;
    const int d_first = (blockIdx.x * (blockDim.x >> 5) + warp) * TW;
    if (d_first >= n_det) return;
    const int d = d_first + lw;
    float acc[C];
#pragma unroll
    for (int k = 0; k < C; ++k) acc[k] = 0.f;
    float weight = 0.f;
    if (d < n_det) {
        const size_t r = (size_t)a * n_det + d;
        weight = ray_weight[r];
        if (weight != 0.f) {
            const float4 ray = rays[r];
            for (int k = lh; k < n_samples; k += TH) {
                float px, py;
                fan_point(ray, (float)k, px, py);
                bilinear_add<C>(img, S, px, py, acc);
            }
        }
    }
#pragma unroll
    for (int offset = TW; offset < 32; offset <<= 1) {
#pragma unroll
        for (int k = 0; k < C; ++k) acc[k] += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffffu, acc[k], offset);
    }
    if (lh == 0 && d < n_det) {
        float* o = out + (size_t)a * n_det + d;
#pragma unroll
        for (int k = 0; k < C; ++k)
            if (k < nb) o[(size_t)k * plane] = acc[k] * weight;
    }
}

// Samples k of a ray whose coordinate p0 + k s lies within one pixel of q, widened by
// TT_WINDOW_SLACK pixels. `inv` is 1 / s, or 0 for a ray that does not move along
// this axis. The window only has to be conservative: the exact forward weights are
// evaluated inside it, and the slack exceeds the rounding of p0 + k s (half an ulp
// of the coordinate, 3e-5 px at 512 px) by a wide margin.
__device__ __forceinline__ void sample_window(float p0, float inv, float q, float& lo, float& hi)
{
    const float reach = 1.f + TT_WINDOW_SLACK;
    if (inv == 0.f) {
        const bool inside = fabsf(p0 - q) < reach;
        lo = inside ? -1e30f : 1e30f;
        hi = inside ? 1e30f : -1e30f;
        return;
    }
    const float t0 = (q - reach - p0) * inv, t1 = (q + reach - p0) * inv;
    lo = fminf(t0, t1);
    hi = fmaxf(t0, t1);
}

// The same for coordinates within `half` + slack of q, covering several pixels at once.
__device__ __forceinline__ void sample_window_reach(float p0, float inv, float q, float half, float& lo, float& hi)
{
    const float reach = half + TT_WINDOW_SLACK;
    if (inv == 0.f) {
        const bool inside = fabsf(p0 - q) < reach;
        lo = inside ? -1e30f : 1e30f;
        hi = inside ? 1e30f : -1e30f;
        return;
    }
    const float t0 = (q - reach - p0) * inv, t1 = (q + reach - p0) * inv;
    lo = fminf(t0, t1);
    hi = fmaxf(t0, t1);
}

// Gather form of the transpose. For each angle the pixel's tent support, the open
// square of half-width one around it, is projected from the source onto the
// detector; only rays in that bin range can have samples inside it, and along each
// such ray only the samples in the matching window. The sinogram arrives already
// multiplied by the ray weights.
//
// Each thread owns PY pixels of one column. They share the candidate rays, the
// sample positions, and the column's tent, and evaluate only the row tent apiece.
template <int C, int PY>
__device__ void fan_adjoint(const float* __restrict__ sino, const float4* __restrict__ rays,
                            const float2* __restrict__ inv_steps, const float4* __restrict__ views,
                            const float* __restrict__ mask, float* __restrict__ out, int S, int A, int n_det,
                            int n_samples, float alpha, float beta, int nb, int plane)
{
    const int j = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
    const int i0 = (blockIdx.y * blockDim.y + threadIdx.y) * PY;
    if (i0 >= S || j >= S) return;
    float m[PY];
    bool any = false;
#pragma unroll
    for (int p = 0; p < PY; ++p) {
        m[p] = (i0 + p < S) ? (mask ? mask[(i0 + p) * S + j] : 1.f) : 0.f;
        any = any || m[p] != 0.f;
    }
    float acc[PY][C];
#pragma unroll
    for (int p = 0; p < PY; ++p)
#pragma unroll
        for (int k = 0; k < C; ++k) acc[p][k] = 0.f;
    if (any) {
        const float jf = (float)j, if0 = (float)i0;
        const float mid = if0 + 0.5f * (float)(PY - 1);
        const float half_y = 1.f + 0.5f * (float)(PY - 1);
        const float last = (float)(n_samples - 1);
        for (int a = 0; a < A; ++a) {
            // v0: source (x, y), detector direction (x, y); v1: detector normal (x, y),
            // then the view's bin offset, (source shift - detector shift) / bin pitch.
            const float4 v0 = views[2 * a], v1 = views[2 * a + 1];
            const float rx = jf - v0.x, ry = mid - v0.y;
            const float lat = rx * v0.z + ry * v0.w, dep = rx * v1.x + ry * v1.y;
            const float offset = beta + v1.z;
            // Bin of each corner of the pixels' joint support, alpha l / e + beta. Far
            // from the source (every default geometry) 1 / (dep + de) is expanded to
            // second order about 1 / dep: de is under two pixels, so beyond 64 px the
            // third-order remainder is far inside the 0.01 bin slack below. Nearer,
            // each corner is divided exactly; a corner at or behind the source plane
            // widens the range to the whole detector, and a support entirely behind
            // it holds no samples at all, since every sample lies in front.
            float dmin = 1e30f, dmax = -1e30f;
            if (dep > 64.f) {
                const float rcp = __frcp_rn(dep);
#pragma unroll
                for (int q = 0; q < 4; ++q) {
                    const float ox = (q & 1) ? 1.f : -1.f, oy = (q & 2) ? half_y : -half_y;
                    const float l = lat + ox * v0.z + oy * v0.w;
                    const float x = (ox * v1.x + oy * v1.y) * rcp;
                    const float dd = l * rcp * (1.f - x + x * x) * alpha + offset;
                    dmin = fminf(dmin, dd);
                    dmax = fmaxf(dmax, dd);
                }
            } else {
                int behind = 0;
#pragma unroll
                for (int q = 0; q < 4; ++q) {
                    const float ox = (q & 1) ? 1.f : -1.f, oy = (q & 2) ? half_y : -half_y;
                    const float l = lat + ox * v0.z + oy * v0.w;
                    const float e = dep + ox * v1.x + oy * v1.y;
                    if (e <= 0.f) {
                        ++behind;
                        continue;
                    }
                    const float dd = l / e * alpha + offset;
                    dmin = fminf(dmin, dd);
                    dmax = fmaxf(dmax, dd);
                }
                if (behind == 4) continue;
                if (behind > 0) {
                    dmin = -1e30f;
                    dmax = 1e30f;
                }
            }
            const int d_lo = (int)fmaxf(0.f, ceilf(dmin - 0.01f));
            const int d_hi = (int)fminf((float)(n_det - 1), floorf(dmax + 0.01f));
            const float* row = sino + (size_t)a * n_det * C;
            for (int d = d_lo; d <= d_hi; ++d) {
                const size_t r = (size_t)a * n_det + d;
                const float4 ray = rays[r];
                const float2 inv = inv_steps[r];
                float xlo, xhi, ylo, yhi;
                sample_window(ray.x, inv.x, jf, xlo, xhi);
                sample_window_reach(ray.y, inv.y, mid, half_y, ylo, yhi);
                const float lo = fmaxf(fmaxf(xlo, ylo), 0.f);
                const float hi = fminf(fminf(xhi, yhi), last);
                if (lo > hi) continue;
                const int k_lo = (int)ceilf(lo);
                const int k_hi = (int)floorf(hi);
                float kw[PY];
#pragma unroll
                for (int p = 0; p < PY; ++p) kw[p] = 0.f;
                for (int k = k_lo; k <= k_hi; ++k) {
                    float px, py;
                    fan_point(ray, (float)k, px, py);
                    const float wx = tent_as_forward(px, jf);
                    const float fy = floorf(py);
                    const float dy = py - fy;
#pragma unroll
                    for (int p = 0; p < PY; ++p) {
                        const float pix = if0 + (float)p;
                        const float wy = (fy == pix) ? 1.f - dy : ((fy + 1.f == pix) ? dy : 0.f);
                        kw[p] += wx * wy;
                    }
                }
                bool hit = false;
#pragma unroll
                for (int p = 0; p < PY; ++p) hit = hit || kw[p] != 0.f;
                if (hit) {
                    float y[C];
                    fetch<C>(row, d, y);
#pragma unroll
                    for (int p = 0; p < PY; ++p)
#pragma unroll
                        for (int k = 0; k < C; ++k) acc[p][k] += kw[p] * y[k];
                }
            }
        }
    }
#pragma unroll
    for (int p = 0; p < PY; ++p) {
        if (i0 + p >= S) break;
        float* o = out + (size_t)(i0 + p) * S + j;
#pragma unroll
        for (int k = 0; k < C; ++k)
            if (k < nb) o[(size_t)k * plane] = acc[p][k] * m[p];
    }
}

// FBP backprojection with the 1/U^2 distance weight, in the normalised coordinates
// of the eager path: source at src (-sin, cos), detector centre at det (sin, -cos),
// each slid along (cos, sin) by its lateral shift. pose[a] = (cos, sin, detector
// shift, source shift). The depth along the detector normal, and so span and U,
// do not move with a lateral shift.
template <int C, bool SHIFTED>
__device__ void fan_backproject(const float* __restrict__ sino,
                                const typename view_table<SHIFTED>::type* __restrict__ pose,
                                const float* __restrict__ coords, const float* __restrict__ mask,
                                float* __restrict__ out, int S, int A, int n_det, float src, float det,
                                float half_width, int nb, int plane, float scale)
{
    const int j = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
    const int i = blockIdx.y * blockDim.y + threadIdx.y;
    if (i >= S || j >= S) return;
    const float m = mask ? mask[i * S + j] : 1.f;
    float acc[C];
#pragma unroll
    for (int k = 0; k < C; ++k) acc[k] = 0.f;
    if (m != 0.f) {
        const float gx = coords[j], gy = coords[i];
        const float half = 0.5f * (float)(n_det - 1);
        const float span = src + det;
        for (int a = 0; a < A; ++a) {
            const float4 t = as_view(pose[a]);
            const float cs = t.x, sn = t.y;
            float src_x = -src * sn, src_y = src * cs;
            float det_x = det * sn, det_y = -det * cs;
            if constexpr (SHIFTED) {
                src_x = __fmaf_rn(t.w, cs, src_x);
                src_y = __fmaf_rn(t.w, sn, src_y);
